# MS-1: Build the compact UI on fixtures in egui

> **Definition, not state.** Authoring drafts live in `.project-pipeline/2026-11-22-ms-1-ui-on-fixtures/authoring/`; generated execution status and evidence pointers live in `.project-pipeline/2026-11-22-ms-1-ui-on-fixtures/execution/`.

## 1. Objective

Deliver the approved compact Fuzzy DeDupe desktop interface (SPEC-FZDP v0.9 §11) as a working egui/eframe application that runs entirely on deterministic fixture data: the shell with its rail and main menu, the Findings and Compare workspaces, and every popup. A simulated scan reports progress and accepts cancellation, Compare shows a real `similar` line diff of two 5 MB fixture texts, the queue enforces the review-first and kept-counterpart rules, and the cleanup dialog runs through review, running, stopped and finished with simulated outcomes.

MS-1 exists to prove, before any real discovery or file operation is built, that egui reaches the MS-1 targets T1 to T4 on the reference workstation and that the implementation matches the review frames. Every item of the MS-1 exit list must hold for the application build the owner signs, so each workstation, design and manual record names the application inputs it validated, and T27 renews or formally reviews any record that predates the signed tree (D-016). MS-1 is complete only when the owner has signed off the evidence file `docs/evidence/ms-1-evidence.md` (T27) and the close-out pull request (T28) has landed with green `main` runs.

The principal invariant is safety by absence: the MS-1 build contains no code that moves or deletes the user's files and no Trash dependency. Two layers enforce it on every pull request: Clippy's `disallowed-methods` and `disallowed-types` lints from a workspace `clippy.toml`, which resolve imports, aliases and re-exports, and a bounded textual guard test (D-006, C-09).

## 3. Scope, Boundaries, and Constraints

### 3.1 In Scope

- Owner-approved record of the MS-1 dependency additions: the named fontconfig lookup crate and any dev-dependency the owner chooses to approve (T1).
- PNG exports of the 19 review frames in `docs/design/reference/` with a manifest (T2).
- A workspace `clippy.toml` whose `disallowed-methods` and `disallowed-types` make the existing `clippy` job reject file-mutation and process-spawn APIs outside the C-09 allowlist, and a workspace test that keeps that configuration intact and textually rejects the same routes and any Trash crate in a manifest or the lockfile (T3).
- MS-1 domain types, the engine's typed request/event contract, and fixture services: result scenarios with 100,000 flattened rows, a simulated scan job, a `similar`-based comparison service with two 5 MB fixture texts, the review/queue policy, and a simulated cleanup run (T4–T9).
- The four MS-1 platform adapters (per-user folders, folder picker, system-font lookup, opening a folder or URL), the app-owned file writer and the age-based expiry of the application's own log files (T10, T21).
- The egui application: theme and tokens, fonts and icons, shell, main menu, dialog host, keyboard map, logging, Findings, the paired diff widget, Compare, all seven popups, and `preferences.toml` (T11–T20).
- The `egui_kittest` snapshot job on the GitHub-hosted Ubuntu runner with Mesa lavapipe, numeric layout assertions, and cross-popup and edge-state integration tests (T22).
- Measurement instrumentation and the T1–T4 runs on the reference workstation (T23, T25).
- The recorded design review (T24), the manual GNOME and KDE Plasma pass (T26), the signed MS-1 evidence file with its freshness review against the signed tree (T27), and the close-out pull request with the `main` proof, the Appendix B.3 completion report, the §17.3 statuses and any `DEV-` rows (T28).

### 3.2 Out of Scope and Deferred

- Real discovery, hashing, decoding, matching, SQLite persistence, file identity, and the Trash backend: MS-2 to MS-5 (§19). MS-1 reads no user file contents.
- Any code that moves, renames or deletes the user's files, and any `trash` dependency (exit item 8; permanent until MS-5 replaces the guard's scope). Writing the application's own preferences and rotating its own logs are allowed (exit item 8).
- Final tokenization and normalization profiles and their defaults: MS-3 (OQ-006). MS-1 applies NFC and the four Ignore options only to display the Normalized view.
- UI check 15, a design-review item that is not an MS-1 exit condition (§8.3). UI checks 09 to 11 are in scope and run against the simulated cleanup (exit item 4).
- Any framework comparison, Slint spike or framework-switch trigger (C-015, ADR 0001 amendment).
- Packaging, release notices beyond the in-repo notice files, and the GUI-independent benchmark harness of §8.5.

### 3.4 Constraints and Authorization

- **Preconditions (MS-0 complete, not task dependencies), named as MS-0 produces them.** PC-1: the eight §8.5 crates exist; the workspace denies `unsafe_code` and every crate root except `fuzzy-dedupe-platform` carries `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; dependency direction holds and the app→platform edge exists. PC-2: `fuzzy-dedupe-app` builds the `fuzzy-dedupe` binary. PC-3: `.github/workflows/rust.yml` runs jobs `fmt`, `clippy`, `test`, `deny`, `audit`, `portability` and `boundaries` on PRs and on `main` (MS-0 C-3); nextest runs with `--no-tests=warn` until MS-1's first test; `deny.toml` lists `GPL-3.0-only` exceptions for the eight workspace crates; the `boundaries` job runs `python3 .github/scripts/check-boundaries.py` against `.github/boundaries.toml` (MS-0 D-011 rules 1–5, C-7), whose third-party table is empty, so any direct dependency without an entry for its declaring crate fails it. PC-4: `.github/workflows/reuse.yml` runs job `reuse` (reuse 6.3.0); `LICENSES/OFL-1.1.txt` and `LICENSES/GPL-3.0-only.txt` exist; `REUSE.toml` follows MS-0 D-008, D-012 and C-5, so no annotation glob matches `*.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `deny.toml`, `.github/scripts/**`, `.github/boundaries.toml` or a header-bearing workflow, and every new comment-capable source or build or CI configuration file carries the two SPDX lines. PC-5: `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/assets/fonts/{IBMPlexSans-Regular,IBMPlexSans-Medium,IBMPlexSans-SemiBold,IBMPlexMono-Regular}.ttf` are present unmodified, with checksums recorded in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (MS-0 C-4), and MS-0 evidence is at `docs/evidence/ms-0-ci-gates.md` and `docs/evidence/ms-0-completion-report.md`. PC-6: when the owner says to start MS-1, the executor creates the governing MS-1 issue with `gh-workflow new` and admits it to `Ready`; under `AGENTS.md` that operator instruction is sufficient authority. PC-7: MS-0 is complete, which MS-0 defines as its close-out PR having landed with green `main` runs (MS-0 T6 and §10.1 step 4), and MS-0's post-landing handoff record (MS-0 §10.1 step 5, D-013) is on `main`; landing the MS-0 topic PR alone is not enough. No task starts until all seven hold.
- **egui is committed** (C-015, ADR 0001). A missed target is fixed within egui through a correction task, or the owner changes the design or the target. The plan contains no framework alternative.
- **Dependencies.** Only crates with a §8.3 approved row may become direct dependencies of any workspace crate, including dev-dependencies. T1's owner-approved PR adds rows for the named fontconfig crate, `unicode-normalization` (NFC and the Normalized view) and `proptest` (development only). Any other new direct dependency (for example `rand`, `rayon`, `tempfile` or an opener crate) requires an owner-approved table row first; an executor who needs one stops and appends a discovered task for the row. The task that first adds an approved crate to a crate's manifest adds that crate's MS-0 C-7 third-party entry in the same change, citing its §8.3 row: an OS-specific crate may be declared only by `fuzzy-dedupe-platform`, and a GUI crate (`egui`, `eframe`, `egui_*`, `winit`) only by `fuzzy-dedupe-app`. Every such task also claims `Cargo.lock` and `.github/boundaries.toml` and lands serially under D-014.
- **Heavy commands run through rexec:** `rexec -- cargo build --workspace --locked`, `rexec -- cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `rexec -- cargo nextest run --workspace --locked`, `rexec -- cargo test --doc --workspace --locked`, `rexec -- cargo deny check`, `rexec -- cargo audit`. Exit codes 20–25 are rexec failures, never the command's result. Parallel executors work in separate Git worktrees; rexec serializes invocations per repository root.
- **Reference-workstation exception.** T21's glyph check and captures, T24's captures and T25's T1–T4 runs execute on the owner's reference workstation (Intel Core Ultra 7 155H, integrated Intel Arc graphics, 62 GB, Fedora with KDE Plasma on Wayland; §11.9), not on the rexec worker, because they need the real display, GPU and system fonts. The measured release binary is built locally on the workstation under the same exception, or built through rexec with `--pull target/release/fuzzy-dedupe` if the pulled binary runs there; the evidence records which.
- **Snapshot runner.** Snapshot baselines are generated and checked only on the GitHub-hosted Ubuntu runner with Mesa lavapipe (§17.2), in a UI-test job MS-1 adds to `.github/workflows/rust.yml`. The repository is public, so GitHub-hosted runners are correct for it.
- **Licensing of new files.** Every new `*.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `clippy.toml` and workflow edit keeps or carries the two SPDX lines (MS-0 D-008); new Markdown carries them inline; new binary or CSV files are covered by a `REUSE.toml` annotation (owner T11) or a per-file `.license` sidecar. `REUSE.toml` annotations follow MS-0 D-012 by origin: Lucide icons are third-party ISC (class d) and the PNG design exports are project-owned `GPL-3.0-only` (class a, §11.1). No MS-1 annotation glob may match a MS-0 C-5 protected path or `clippy.toml`. The `reuse` job passes on every PR.
- **Git and landing.** Git-mutating steps run locally. Non-handoff changes land through a draft PR and `.agents/skills/github-workflow/bin/gh-workflow land --pr N --method squash` (D-009). `admission` runs only on pushes to `main`, so no pull-request acceptance expects it. A task becomes `done`, and a dependent task branches from `main`, only after its squash commit shows green `main` runs of `rust.yml`, `reuse.yml`, `format`, `lint-markdown`, `validate-specs` and `admission`. Task branches are never deleted, locally or on origin, before the T28 close-out PR has landed and its post-landing handoff record is on `main`, because `recover` finds checkpoints with `git log --all`. Handoff-only updates use `Workflow-Admission: handoff` commits outside the task graph.
- **Not authorized:** any operation on user files beyond reading folder metadata in New scan; editing the ADRs, `.gitignore`, `.claude/settings.json`, `deny.toml`, `.github/scripts/check-boundaries.py`, `.github/workflows/reuse.yml`, or any `rust.yml` job other than the `--no-tests=warn` removal (T3) and the added UI-test job (T22); editing `.github/boundaries.toml` beyond adding third-party entries for crates the task itself adds; a wholesale `cargo update`; publishing releases; changing an acceptance threshold without an owner decision recorded in the spec.

## 4. Current State and Target State

### 4.1 Current State

Before MS-0 the repository holds documentation only: SPEC-FZDP v0.9, ADR 0001, handoff documents, the pinned toolchain (`rust-toolchain.toml`, Rust 1.96.0), `.rexec.toml` with nextest, cargo-deny and cargo-audit, and CI for Markdown formatting and lint, spec validation and commit admission. No Cargo workspace, crate, test or design export exists, and `docs/design/` does not exist. MS-0, planned separately, produces the workspace with a `Cargo.lock` listing only the eight workspace packages, `rust.yml`, `deny.toml`, `.github/scripts/check-boundaries.py` and `.github/boundaries.toml`, `reuse.yml`, `REUSE.toml`, `LICENSES/`, the Plex fonts, `docs/evidence/ms-0-ci-gates.md` and `docs/evidence/ms-0-completion-report.md` listed in §3.4. No `clippy.toml` exists. Every PR so far has squash-landed; `main` has no branch protection or ruleset, and `delete_branch_on_merge` is false.

### 4.2 Target State

The `fuzzy-dedupe` binary opens a 1600 × 960 "Fuzzy DeDupe" window (minimum 1024 × 600) that implements §11 against fixtures: the rail with + New scan, Findings, Compare and Menu; the main menu; both workspaces; and all seven popups, in light and dark themes that follow the system with an override. Fixture scenarios cover the review frames' sample data, a 100,000-row result set, edge states, and two 5 MB texts. View preferences persist in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/fuzzy-dedupe/preferences.toml`; logs roll daily in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/fuzzy-dedupe/logs`, and the application deletes its own log files once their date is more than 7 days old. CI adds an `egui_kittest` snapshot job, and the existing `clippy` job enforces the workspace `clippy.toml`. `docs/design/reference/` holds the review-frame PNGs, and `docs/evidence/ms-1-evidence.md` records every exit item with the owner's sign-off.

### 4.3 Delta Summary

| Application | MS-0 skeleton binary | Full §11 UI on fixtures | No file moves or deletes; no Trash dependency |
| Engine/domain | MS-0 empty crates | MS-1 domain types, request/event contract, fixture services | Domain depends on no workspace crate and no GUI type |
| Platform | MS-0 empty crate | Four MS-1 adapters plus the app-owned file writer | OS-specific code and dependencies only here (NFR-008) |
| CI | `rust.yml` seven jobs (`fmt`, `clippy`, `test`, `deny`, `audit`, `portability`, `boundaries`) and `reuse.yml` from MS-0 | `--no-tests=warn` removed; UI-test job added to `rust.yml`; `clippy.toml` enforced by the existing `clippy` job; C-7 third-party entries for every MS-1 crate | Every MS-0 job stays green and keeps its ID |
| Docs | Spec, ADR, handoff | Reference PNGs, evidence file, completion report, §8.3 rows, §17.3 statuses | Spec IDs and ADR text unchanged |

### 5.2 Control / Data / State Flow

```text
widgets (draw only) --emit--> UiIntent queue --app.rs, after the UI pass-->
   review/queue commands ----sync, in-memory----> engine review service (C-05) --> outcome
   job commands (scan, compare, cleanup) --bounded crossbeam channel--> engine workers
engine workers --revisioned events, bounded--> app.rs drains a bounded batch per frame
   --> presentation state owners (§11.8) --> next frame draws
platform adapters <-- app.rs / dialogs (dirs, picker, open location, fonts, app-owned writes)
```

Widgets never call the engine, the platform or the filesystem while drawing. Every job event carries the scan ID, request ID or plan revision that originated it, and the presentation layer discards any event whose origin is no longer current (§11.8). The engine performs no filesystem access in MS-1: fixture contents are generated in memory, and the simulated cleanup operates on IDs only.

| Documentation | yes | §8.3 rows; §17.3 statuses and `DEV-` rows; reference manifest; completion report | PV-T1-001, PV-T28-001 | T1, T28 |
| Durable evidence | yes | Evidence file, supporting records bound to application inputs, renewals (Appendix C, C-14) | PV-T27-001, PV-T27-002 | T21, T22, T24–T28 |

### 5.4 Binding Decisions

| ID | Decision | Rationale | Source | Affected Task(s) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-001 | Build with egui/eframe on wgpu; pin eframe, egui, egui_extras, egui_table and egui_kittest exactly to one mutually compatible set. eframe persistence stays off; egui's bundled default fonts are disabled; the unmodified monochrome Noto Emoji (OFL-1.1) from a pinned release is embedded as the emoji fallback, and fontconfig covers other scripts. | C-015, ADR 0001, DR-009, §8.3, §11.7 Fonts (v0.9) | spec §8.3, §11.7, ADR 0001 | T11, T21 |
| D-002 | Fixture data, the simulated scan, the comparison service and the simulated cleanup live in `fuzzy-dedupe-engine` behind the typed contract C-02. Rejected: a fixture module inside the app crate, because §8.5 and §11.8 forbid UI code running diffs or owning job lifecycles, and MS-4 would have to move it. | §8.2, §8.5, §11.8, §11.9 | spec §8.5, §11.8 | T4–T9, T12 |
| D-003 | The engine review service is the only authority that evaluates queue entry points and owns the scan-scoped decision set; presentation `QueueState` is a read model updated from its outcomes. Rejected: policy in widgets or presentation, because UI check 09 requires one policy across entry points and §8.5 routes file-operation decisions through the application service. | §8.5, §11.8, DR-008 ownership, UI check 09 | spec §11.8, §7.4 | T31, T13, T15, T17 |
| D-004 | Contract-first stubs: T4, T10 and T12 create every module file later tasks fill, so parallel tasks write disjoint files. Rejected: each task adding its own `mod` lines, which would serialize all view and engine tasks through shared files. | Parallel safety | plan method | T4, T10, T12 |
| D-005 | Fixture "files" are deterministic in-memory buffers; MS-1 writes no fixture file at runtime. Rejected: temporary files, which would need deletion code (exit item 8). | Exit item 8 | spec §19 | T4, T6 |
| D-006 | Exit item 8 is enforced in two layers. The semantic layer is a workspace `clippy.toml` (T3) whose `disallowed-methods` and `disallowed-types` list the C-09 routes; the existing MS-0 `clippy` job (`-D warnings`) enforces it with no workflow edit, and because Clippy resolves paths it catches imported, aliased and re-exported uses. An allowed use carries `#[expect(clippy::disallowed_methods, reason = "…")]` or `#[expect(clippy::disallowed_types, reason = "…")]` and appears only where C-09 allows it; `expect` rather than `allow` makes a stale allowance fail as an unfulfilled expectation. The second layer is a bounded textual workspace test in the existing nextest gate, which also keeps `clippy.toml` complete and the allowances confined, and checks manifests and `Cargo.lock` for a Trash crate. Rejected: a textual scan alone, which misses ordinary Rust import and alias forms (round-1 PA-005); a one-off sign-off grep (no regression protection); and a new CI job (MS-0 owns `rust.yml` jobs). Reopen if the owner approves a dedicated lint tool. | Exit item 8; ownership; coordinator decision | spec §19, `request` | T3, T10, T12 |
| D-007 | Snapshot tests are isolated from the seven MS-0 jobs and run in a UI-test job added to `rust.yml` on a pinned GitHub-hosted Ubuntu image with Mesa lavapipe; baselines come only from that runner, and the job fails when zero snapshot tests execute. The snapshot harness uses embedded fonts only. Rejected: workstation or rexec baselines and real system fallback fonts, both of which differ from the runner and make baselines unstable; and a separate workflow file, because MS-0's contract places the UI-test job in `rust.yml`. | §17.2; MS-0 C-3 | spec §17.2 | T22 |
| D-008 | Review commands are synchronous in-memory calls dispatched by `app.rs` after the UI pass; scan, comparison and cleanup are asynchronous jobs over bounded channels with revisioned events. Rejected: routing cheap review commands through worker threads, which adds ordering hazards without I/O to hide. | §11.8, §11.9 | spec §11.8 | T4, T31, T12 |
| D-009 | Landing: one draft PR per task by default; consecutive dependent tasks finished by one executor may share a PR of at most three task commits, except that a D-014 task shares a PR with no other D-014 task. Land with `gh-workflow land --pr N --method squash`, matching MS-0 and every earlier PR. Task-branch commits carry only the `Plan-*` checkpoint trailers and never a self-written `Workflow-Admission` trailer; `merge` writes `Workflow-Admission: PR #N` into the squash commit. The branch commit is the checkpoint recorded with `state --commit`, and the executor requests `done` only after the squash commit's `main` runs of `rust.yml`, `reuse.yml`, `format`, `lint-markdown`, `validate-specs` and `admission` are green, so no dependent task branches from an unproven `main`. `admission` runs only on pushes to `main`; PR acceptance never expects it. `recover` scans `git log --all`, so no task branch is deleted, locally or on origin, until the T28 close-out PR has landed and its post-landing handoff record is on `main` (`delete_branch_on_merge` is false), and T28 records the checkpoint OID, PR number and squash OID of T1–T27 and completed corrections, while T28's own go to the post-landing handoff record on `main`. Rejected: merge-method landing with self-written admission trailers, because the pr-standard makes `merge` the owner of that trailer and MS-0's probe showed merge and rebase landings leave commits unadmitted. | `recover` discovers checkpoints through `git log --all`; pr-standard admission ownership; MS-0 D-010; coordinator decision | `request`, `repo:AGENTS.md`, pr-standard | all tasks |
| D-010 | Measurement samples are printed to standard output as CSV by a release build in an opt-in measurement mode; the operator redirects them into the evidence directory. Rejected: in-app file writing, which would widen the file-writing allowlist. | Exit item 8; §11.9 | spec §11.9 | T23, T25 |
| D-011 | No crate beyond the §8.3 rows. T1 adds the `unicode-normalization` row, used by T6 for NFC and the Normalized view, and the development-only `proptest` row, used by T6 for normalization properties. Queue invariants keep exhaustive enumeration over bounded group shapes as their oracle, because enumeration covers every small shape deterministically; the owner's 2026-11-23 bound (T31) adds seeded deep walks from a hand-rolled generator, still without `proptest`. | §8.3, §17.2; coordinator decision; owner decision 2026-11-23 | spec §8.3, `request` | T1, T6, T31 |
| D-012 | The §8.5 fourth adapter, opening a folder or web address with the desktop's default application, spawns `xdg-open` through `std::process::Command` in the platform crate only; it serves Open containing folder, Open log folder and About's Source code. Rejected: an opener crate, which has no approved row. | §8.5 MS-1 adapters (v0.9); FR-028 | spec §8.5 | T10, T19 |
| D-013 | The MS-1 evidence file is `docs/evidence/ms-1-evidence.md`. It sits beside MS-0's `docs/evidence/ms-0-ci-gates.md`, and T28's `docs/evidence/ms-1-completion-report.md` sits beside MS-0's completion report. Supporting records and raw data live in `docs/evidence/ms-1/`; evidence Markdown carries inline SPDX lines and each binary or CSV file a `.license` sidecar, so evidence tasks never edit the root `REUSE.toml`. | §19 exit list requires one evidence file; Appendix B.3; MS-0 D-010 | request | T21, T22, T24–T28 |
| D-014 | `Cargo.lock` and `.github/boundaries.toml` are shared files. Every task that adds a direct dependency to a manifest, or changes a manifest in a way that changes the resolution (T4, T6, T10, T11, T21, T22), claims both, declares `conflicts_with` against every other such task, and lands serially. Before landing, that task rebases on the current `main`, updates the lock minimally by a resolution run without `--locked` (for example `cargo metadata --format-version 1` or `cargo check`; never a wholesale `cargo update`), reviews the lock diff so that it adds only the new crates and their transitive closure and changes no unrelated version, adds the C-7 entry for each crate it adds, and passes the `--locked` gates and the `boundaries` job against the combined manifests. T4 is the declared owner of both files for MS-1; the others modify only their own entries and lock delta. A later task that adds an approved crate to a further crate (for example `tracing` in the engine) is a D-014 task and appends the same claims. Rejected: one serialized integration task that adds every dependency up front, because it would declare crates before their consumers exist and push lock review away from the change that needs it; and independent lock regeneration per branch, which validates a resolution different from the combined one (round-1 PA-006). | §8.3 committed lockfile; §17.2 locked builds; MS-0 C-7; coordinator decision | spec §8.3, §17.2, `request` | T4, T6, T10, T11, T21, T22 |
| D-015 | Log retention is age-based. At startup and at each daily rollover the application deletes its own log files, matching only the roller's file-name pattern inside the platform-resolved log directory, whose file-name date is more than 7 days before the current date in the roller's time base; recent logs, files that do not match, files with an unparseable date, directories and symbolic links are kept. The deletion lives in `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-platform/src/log_retention.rs` (T10), the only module C-09 allows to delete, and takes the current date as a parameter so tests inject the clock; T12 calls it outside the UI pass. A file-count cap on the roller may stay as an extra bound only. Rejected: a count cap alone, because intermittent use makes seven daily files span far more than seven days (round-1 PA-002); and deletion inside the app crate, which would put a delete call outside the platform confinement the guard checks. | NFR-009 "kept for 7 days"; exit item 8 "rotating its own logs is allowed"; coordinator decision | spec NFR-009, §18.5, §19, `request` | T10, T12, T3 |
| D-016 | Evidence freshness: every durable workstation, design, manual or CI record (EV-001 to EV-004, EV-006) names the application commit OID it validated and, at that commit, the tree OID of `crates/` and the object IDs of `Cargo.lock`, the root `Cargo.toml` and `rust-toolchain.toml` (C-14). T24, T25 and T26 depend on every runtime implementation task, T20 and T21 included. T27 compares each record's binding with the signed tree; on a mismatch it reruns the affected checks or, only for a change it shows to be non-functional (documentation, comments, test-only), records an equivalence review naming the diff, both in EV-007. Every correction task lists the evidence records it invalidates. The root `Cargo.toml` and `rust-toolchain.toml` are bound as well as the two inputs the coordinator named because a release-profile or toolchain change alters the measured binary without touching `crates/`. Rejected: trusting a historical pass because its task is `done`, which lets sign-off rest on a different build (round-1 PA-001). | §19 exit items 1–3, 6, 7 and owner sign-off; §11.9; coordinator decision | spec §19, §11.9, `request` | T21, T22, T24–T27, correction tasks |

## 7. Verification and Evidence Strategy

- **Authoritative commands:** `rexec -- cargo fmt --all --check`; `rexec -- cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`; `rexec -- cargo nextest run --workspace --locked`; `rexec -- cargo test --doc --workspace --locked`; `rexec -- cargo deny check`; `rexec -- cargo audit`; `reuse lint`; the repository's Prettier and markdownlint commands from `AGENTS.md` for changed Markdown; the MS-0 `portability` check and `python3 .github/scripts/check-boundaries.py` (the `boundaries` job). These mirror the `rust.yml` and `reuse.yml` jobs; the UI-test job runs only in GitHub Actions, and `admission` runs only on pushes to `main`, so it is observed after landing and never as PR acceptance. The `clippy` command enforces the workspace `clippy.toml` once T3 lands. Every task ends with these green, and a task is `done` only after its squash commit's `main` runs are green (D-009).
- **Oracles:** spec tables are the oracle for geometry, tokens, labels, keyboard map and popup contents; generator-declared edit scripts are the oracle for diff alignment and unique counts; exhaustive state enumeration is the oracle for queue invariants; byte equality with the generated buffer is the oracle for copy; the reference PNGs are the oracle for the design review; spec §11.9 thresholds are the oracle for T1–T4.
- **Negative controls:** temporary Clippy canaries in fully qualified, imported, aliased, re-exported, multiline and `#[cfg(test)]` forms, and textual canary snippets, for the no-mutation guard; log files on both sides of the 7-day boundary with an unrelated file; each decision kind against each binding component with unchanged-binding controls; an or-later grant injected into About's own summary; a deliberately mismatched token value and a 1-unit layout offset beyond tolerance; queue attempts on reference, protected, kept, counterpart-queued and unacknowledged rows; stale comparison responses; a second confirmation submission; a cross-pane selection attempt; a zero-test snapshot run.
- **Test layers:** unit (domain, policy, generators), deterministic enumeration (queue invariants), contract (request/event revisions), headless `egui_kittest` interaction tests on any machine, `egui_kittest` snapshots on the lavapipe runner only, workstation-only measurement and glyph checks, and manual owner-performed desktop acceptance.
- **External environments:** GitHub-hosted Ubuntu with Mesa lavapipe, in the `rust.yml` UI-test job (T22); the reference workstation with KDE Plasma on Wayland (T21, T24, T25), with two monitors at different scales for T26's KDE Plasma half; a Fedora Workstation virtual machine with GNOME on Wayland, supplied by the owner, for T26's GNOME half; Figma access or the owner for exports (T2).
- **Evidence:** repeatable test output is ephemeral. Workstation, manual, CI-citation and approval-bearing results are durable records `EV-001` to `EV-008` (Appendix C). Each of EV-001 to EV-004 and EV-006 starts with its C-14 binding block, so T27 can prove it describes the signed application (D-016). Evidence never contains user names, host names, home paths or other personal data.
- **Late failure:** a failed integration, measurement, review or manual check blocks that verification task. The executor appends a correction task with `corrects:` and `discovered_from:` against the owning task, states in its `invariants` which evidence records it invalidates (`invalidates: [EV-…]`, or `invalidates: none` with the reason), completes it, and reruns the blocked check. A correction that lands after a record's task is `done` leaves that record stale until T27 renews it or records its equivalence review (D-016). A T1–T4 miss that egui remediation cannot fix goes to the owner, who changes the design or the target in the spec before the plan is revised.

#### T32: Make the no-file-mutation guard terminate on precise-capturing `use<…>`

- **disposition:** active
- **outcome:** the textual guard in `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/no_file_mutation.rs` recognizes a `use` declaration only where an item can start, never treats Rust's precise-capturing bound (`impl Trait + use<'a>`, `use<>`) as one, and terminates in time linear in the scanned token count on every input, including unbalanced braces and generated pathological sources; every C-09 textual and configuration rule it enforced at T3's checkpoint still rejects the same canaries, and a bounded-time regression test fails, rather than hangs, when termination regresses
- **work_type:** behavior
- **checkpoint:** one green commit with the required `Plan-*` checkpoint trailers, landed by squash
- **boundary:** cross-task
- **depends_on:** [T3]
- **dependency_reason:** T32 corrects the T3-owned guard test `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/no_file_mutation.rs`
- **requirements:** [REQ-008]
- **proof:** [PV-T32-001]
- **source_refs:** [request, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#19-implementation-plan, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#85-design-constraints]
- **consumes:** [C-09]
- **produces:** [a bounded-time C-09 textual layer, with no change to C-09's rules]
- **preserves:** [C-09 rule set, allowlist and test-code policy unchanged; every existing test in the file kept with its assertions; `clippy.toml`, `rust.yml` and every manifest unchanged]
- **invariants:** [discovered from T18: on 2026-11-23 `no_file_mutation::workspace_is_clean` ran past 180 s and was killed by SIGKILL when a scanned source contained `+ use<'a>`, while the same tree without the construct finished in about 0.3 s, so any later code using the syntax would hang the CI `test` job; a reading of T3's checkpoint suggests the mechanism, which T32.1 confirms or refutes and which is not a binding diagnosis: `use_hits` treats every `use` identifier as a declaration and ends its range at the next `;` at brace depth ≤ 0, which can run past the `}` closing the enclosing block, and `parse_use_tree` stops at `}` without advancing, so the caller's loop can revisit the same token forever; the same over-long range would also hide the function body from the qualified-path pass, a false negative; after T32, every loop in the use-statement and use-tree scan consumes at least one token per iteration, a `use` token followed by `<` is never a declaration, and a declaration's range never extends past the `}` that closes its enclosing block; invalidates: none, because no MS-1 evidence record exists yet and the change is test-only]
- **executor_discretion:** [the item-position recognition technique, the deadline mechanism of the bounded-time test (a worker thread with a timed receive, or a step counter asserted against the token count), fixture content, test names, failure message wording]
- **files:** [`crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/no_file_mutation.rs` (modify; owner T3)]
- **parallel_safe:** yes
- **conflicts_with:** [T3]
- **supersedes:** []
- **superseded_by:** []
- **corrects:** [T3]
- **discovered_from:** [T18]
- **evidence:** [ephemeral]
- **recovery:** revert to the last green checkpoint; the guard adds no production code
- **acceptance:** PV-T32-001 proves (1) that scans of fixtures containing `-> impl Trait + use<'a>` and `+ use<>` in return position inside an `impl` block and at module level, `use` declarations inside `macro_rules!` bodies and inline modules, a `use` token before an unbalanced `}`, and a generated source of at least 10,000 such constructs each finish within a fixed deadline, and that the bounded-time test fails, not hangs, when the deadline passes; (2) that a C-09 qualified path (`std::fs::remove_file(…)`) in the body of a function whose return type carries `+ use<…>`, and a C-09 `use` declaration that follows such a function, are each still flagged; (3) that every PV-T3-001 canary and acceptance case gives its T3 result, with the existing tests unmodified; (4) that `workspace_is_clean` passes on the clean workspace, and on a scratch copy with a `+ use<'a>` function added to an existing scanned source file, in under 10 s; and (5) that the repository gates are green
- **sub-tasks:**
  - **T32.1 RED** — add the bounded-time fixtures of acceptance (1) and the mixed-form canaries of acceptance (2); expected failures: the `use<>` fixtures exceed the deadline and, if the hypothesis holds, the body path beside `use<…>` is not flagged.
  - **T32.2 Verify RED** — run `rexec -- cargo nextest run -p fuzzy-dedupe-app --locked -E 'test(no_file_mutation)'` and confirm the failures are the deadline and missing detection, not build errors, and that the test process ends on its own deadline.
  - **T32.3 GREEN** — make the use-statement and use-tree scan recognize declarations only at item position, skip precise-capturing `use<…>`, bound each declaration's range by its enclosing block, and guarantee progress on every loop iteration.
  - **T32.4 Verify GREEN** — rerun the targeted tests, the full nextest gate and the Clippy gate, and time `workspace_is_clean` on the scratch copy of acceptance (4).
  - **T32.5 REFACTOR** — assess and record none.
  - **T32.6 Verify Task** — run PV-T32-001 and the repository gates; commit with the required `Plan-*` trailers.

- **consumes:** [C-01, C-02, engine module stubs]

- **invariants:** [C-05 invariants I-1 to I-10 hold after every operation sequence; supersedes T7: on 2026-11-23 the owner chose "bound plus seeded deep walks", replacing PV-T7-001's bound (every group shape up to 5 members, every operation sequence up to depth 6) with PV-T31-001's tiered enumeration bound and seeded depth-24 walks; the decision rests on the T7 worker's release-build measurement on the rexec worker the same day, in which the 2,912 five-member shapes took 33 s at depth 2 and extrapolate to about 330 CPU-hours at depth 6 with tens of GB of state per shape, the 108-operation five-member alphabet already holds one representative per branch, so no sound abstraction shrinks it, and the tiered bound is estimated at about 211 s in release; D-011 stands: the walks use a hand-rolled seeded generator, not `proptest`]
- **executor_discretion:** [internal data structures, refusal message wording within §11.4 labels]
- **files:** [`crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/src/review/mod.rs` (modify; owner T4), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/src/review/policy.rs` (create; owner T31), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/tests/review_policy.rs` (test; owner T31), `.github/workflows/rust.yml` (modify: add one release-mode enumeration step to the `test` job only; owner T3)]
- **parallel_safe:** yes
- **conflicts_with:** [T3, T4, T22]
- **supersedes:** [T7]
- **superseded_by:** []
- **evidence:** [ephemeral]
- **recovery:** revert to the last green checkpoint; the stub remains in place
- **acceptance:** PV-T31-001 proves (1) by exhaustive enumeration, in a release-mode step of the `rust.yml` `test` job, that I-1 to I-4 hold for every group shape of up to 3 members and every operation sequence up to depth 6, for every 4-member shape and every sequence up to depth 4, and for every 5-member shape and every sequence up to depth 2; (2) by seeded walks in the debug test gate, at least 50 of depth 24 for every 5-member group shape, that I-1 to I-4 hold after every step, each walk drawn by a hand-rolled generator from a fixed seed (D-011), so every run replays the same walks and a failure names its shape, seed and step; (3) that the oracle of both is independent of the service: it keeps its own model of decisions, their bindings, queue eligibility and the expected invalidation lists, and after every step asserts that the service's state and outcome equal the model's exact expected values, never accepting whatever the service chooses; (4) that in both queue orders queuing a file whose counterpart is queued is refused with "Counterpart queued" and queuing a file that a queued file was measured against is refused with "Counterpart of a queued file", in both cases leaving the queue unchanged; and (5) that the debug workspace gate skips the enumeration and the release step runs it, failing when its filter matches no test; PV-T31-002 proves Kept and Protected follow file IDs across sort-independent queries, no operation queues a protected file, and Queue identical copies skips kept, protected, reference and non-byte-identical members, and that Keep both records a Kept decision for both displayed files and clears pending removal intent for that pair (unqueues either displayed file that is queued), also when the pair contains the reference, leaving every other group member's decisions and queue state unchanged (spec §11.5); PV-T31-003 proves, for each decision kind (Kept, per-file Protect, acknowledgment) and each binding component (target snapshot, counterpart snapshot, rules revision), that a fixture-injected change of that component alone invalidates that decision and no decision with an unchanged binding; that a counterpart change through Make this the reference or a Compare review invalidates the decisions bound to the old counterpart; that an evidence update repeating the current values, or changing only another member, leaves every decision intact; that a queued target whose acknowledgment or approval is invalidated leaves the queue; that `ClearForNewScan` empties every decision of every kind; and that folder protection from the scan setup survives every binding change and the new scan, and also that a Compare queue request naming a counterpart other than the row's current one, including for a file that is already queued, is evaluated against that counterpart: it runs the normal refusal and review gates, a refusal leaves state unchanged, and otherwise the counterpart change invalidates the decisions bound to the old counterpart (§10.1, C-05 I-7 and I-8) and the outcome is queued or needs review as the review gate decides, never the already-queued result against the old counterpart; PV-T31-004 proves Make this the reference on a queued member removes it from the queue, recomputes every counterpart by the §10.1 rule while discarding Compare-chosen counterparts, unqueues each other queued member whose counterpart changed and clears its acknowledgment, leaves members with an unchanged counterpart untouched, and returns the unqueued count
- **sub-tasks:**
  - **T31.1 RED** — add the independent oracle model, the tiered enumeration under `review_policy::enumeration`, marked `#[ignore]` with a reason naming the release step, the seeded depth-24 walks, both FR-025 queue orders, and the PV-T31-002 to PV-T31-004 cases, including Keep both and the Compare request that names a new counterpart; expected failure: stub returns not implemented.
  - **T31.2 Verify RED** — run `rexec -- cargo nextest run -p fuzzy-dedupe-engine --locked -E 'test(review_policy)'` and `rexec -- cargo nextest run -p fuzzy-dedupe-engine --release --locked --run-ignored only -E 'test(review_policy::enumeration)'`, and confirm the failures are the stub, not build errors.
  - **T31.3 GREEN** — implement the service; add one step after `Run tests` in the `rust.yml` `test` job that runs `cargo nextest run -p fuzzy-dedupe-engine --release --locked --run-ignored only -E 'test(review_policy::enumeration)'`, leaving every job ID and existing step unchanged.
  - **T31.4 Verify GREEN** — rerun both commands and the engine tests, and record the release enumeration's wall time in the task evidence.
  - **T31.5 REFACTOR** — assess and record none.
  - **T31.6 Verify Task** — run PV-T31-001 to PV-T31-004 and repository gates, and confirm the PR's `rust.yml` `test` job runs and passes the enumeration step; commit with the required `Plan-*` trailers.

#### T33: Complete the presentation hub for the wave-3 views

- **disposition:** active
- **outcome:** the presentation read model mirrors what the review service reports, and views read shared state instead of private copies: `QueueState` records each file's current role, and with it its counterpart, from every review outcome, so Findings shows a counterpart chosen in Compare on an unqueued row; Queue identical copies states the count and size of exactly the files the service then queues, both computed by one pure eligibility forecast exported by the engine review module; a bulk Queue identical copies result is reported in the status bar; views read the open `MenuKind` through `WorkspaceCx`; adopting the design scenario installs the sample's group titles; and the Findings table records its scroll offset in `FindingsState` and applies it when a view is restored
- **work_type:** behavior
- **checkpoint:** one green commit with the required `Plan-*` checkpoint trailers, landed by squash
- **boundary:** cross-task
- **depends_on:** [T12, T13, T17, T31]
- **dependency_reason:** T33 corrects T12's presentation core and the T13 and T17 fills of T12-owned view files, so it starts after T13 and T17 have landed; it moves the eligibility rule out of T31's `queue_identical` into an exported forecast; T33 lands before T15 starts (`conflicts_with` serializes them, and T15's definition is unchanged), because both claim `presentation/state.rs` and `presentation/projections.rs`, and T15 reads the `WorkspaceCx` menu and the recorded scroll offset
- **requirements:** [REQ-014, REQ-033, REQ-037, REQ-038, IR-001]
- **proof:** [PV-T33-001, PV-T33-002, PV-T33-003, PV-T33-004, PV-T33-005]
- **source_refs:** [request, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#1111-acceptance-checklist-for-the-implementing-agent, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#114-findings-table-structure-and-interactions, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#116-popup-contracts-and-compact-progress-handling, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#118-recommended-rust-ui-modules-and-state-ownership, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#grouping-policy, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#73-interface-requirements, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#111-authoritative-design-links-and-revision-precedence]
- **consumes:** [C-01, C-05, C-12, Findings workspace, review popups, design scenario]
- **produces:** [a queue-identical eligibility forecast, a pure read-only function that extends C-05 with no new operation, outcome or state; per-file roles in `QueueState`; the open menu in `WorkspaceCx`; the recorded Findings scroll offset]
- **preserves:** [C-05 operations, outcomes and invariants I-1 to I-10 unchanged, with PV-T31-001 to PV-T31-004 passing unmodified; D-003: the review service stays the only queue authority and `QueueState` stays a read model; C-12 at most one overlay; the Compare fields of `state.rs` and the Compare projections untouched (T15 owns them); every popup's behavior outside Queue identical copies unchanged; the status behavior of single-file queue results unchanged]
- **invariants:** [discovered from T13 and T17 on 2026-11-23 and, for the scroll offset, from T13's return: `events::apply_review` dropped `FileReviewState::role`, so T13 derived roles from decisions and queue entries, and a counterpart chosen in Compare on an unqueued row never reached Findings; T17's popup copies the service's eligibility rule and can over-count by one when an unqueued row was re-aimed in Compare onto a non-reference member; a bulk `Queued { files }` result is not reported; `WorkspaceCx` carries no overlay state, so T13 mirrors the open menu in `FindingsState::menu`; `app.rs` never installs `design::sample().group_titles`; T13's `grouped_table.rs` never writes `FindingsState::scroll_offset`, one of the seven IR-001 properties, and T15, which owns the return routing, cannot edit that file; after T33 the app crate holds no queue-eligibility condition, and the popup's count, file set and size come only from the forecast; the service computes the set it queues by evaluating the forecast on its own pre-command state and queues exactly that set, so the two cannot diverge; every read-model input of the forecast (roles, Kept and per-file Protect decisions, protected files, the queue with its counterparts, byte-verified edges) is mirrored from outcomes, the published view or the immutable results; a recorded role is replaced only by an outcome row naming that file and is reset by a new scan's results; the open menu's identity is read only from `WorkspaceCx`, and any Findings-local menu sub-state resets whenever that menu is not a Findings menu; group titles are design-scenario fixture data only (§11.1), and other scenarios keep the default header title; the scroll offset is recorded in logical units from the table's own scroll area and applied once when a view is restored, never forced on every frame; the bulk status line follows the existing status-line form; generated review histories come from a hand-rolled seeded generator, not `proptest` (D-011); invalidates: none, because no MS-1 evidence record exists yet]
- **executor_discretion:** [the forecast's signature (a facts trait implemented by the service state and by the read model, or a plain input struct) and its file inside the review module, how `apply_review` tells the bulk command's result from a single queue result, the bulk status wording within the existing form, how a restore is signalled to the table, test organization and names, history length and seed count above the acceptance minimum]
- **files:** [`crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/src/review/forecast.rs` (create; owner T33), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/src/review/mod.rs` (modify: module declaration and re-export only; owner T4), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/src/review/policy.rs` (modify `queue_identical` to queue exactly the forecast's set only; owner T31), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/presentation/state.rs` (modify Findings and Queue fields only; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/presentation/events.rs` (modify `apply_review` and its status reporting; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/presentation/projections.rs` (modify Findings projections only; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/ui/mod.rs` (modify `WorkspaceCx` only; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/ui/shell.rs` (modify `WorkspaceCx` construction only; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/ui/findings.rs` (modify; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/ui/widgets/grouped_table.rs` (modify: roles, menu reads and the scroll offset; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/ui/dialogs/queue_identical.rs` (modify: count and size from the forecast; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/app.rs` (modify `adopt_scenario` only; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/findings.rs` (modify assertions on the replaced workarounds; owner T13), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/review_popups.rs` (modify assertions on the replaced forecast; owner T17), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/presentation_hub.rs` (test; owner T33)]

- **acceptance:** PV-T33-001 proves that after each review outcome `QueueState` holds the reported role of every row the outcome names, and that Findings labels and Match and Unique values follow it: a Compare review that re-aims an unqueued row onto a non-reference member makes that row name the member, Make this the reference moves the anchor and relabels each changed counterpart, a new scan resets roles to the new results, and no Findings projection derives a role from decisions or queue entries; PV-T33-002 proves (1) over at least 200 seeded review histories of at least 40 steps each on the design and edge fixture scenarios, mixing queue requests, acknowledgments, Kept, Protect, Make this the reference, Compare re-aims, Keep both, unqueue and both Queue identical copies scopes, that immediately before every Queue identical copies command the count, file set and total size the popup computes from the read model equal the service's `Queued { files }` count, the set of files the published view then newly queues and their total size; (2) that the popup displays that count and size and its Queue N files button names the same N; (3) that a bulk result of N > 0 files sets one status line stating N; and (4) that a search of the app crate finds no eligibility condition outside calls to the forecast; PV-T33-003 proves that the Findings Sort, Actions and row menus open and close through the shared overlay slot, that a menu the dialog host closes (Esc, outside click) is seen closed by the view on the next frame, and that the view reads the open menu only from `WorkspaceCx`, with `FindingsState` holding no copy of which menu is open; PV-T33-004 proves the design scenario's group header rows show the sample's group titles of frame `13:648` after adoption, and a non-design scenario shows the default titles; PV-T33-005 proves that scrolling the table records its offset in `FindingsState`, that restoring a view with a recorded offset shows the table at that offset on the first restored frame, and that the offset survives the round trip exactly; and the repository gates are green

#### T34: Add the per-dialog size hook and one icon-glyph registry

- **disposition:** active
- **outcome:** the dialog host takes each popup's initial size from a per-dialog size hook beside T18's title hook, so New scan opens at 640 × 528 and grows to 640 × 778 with matching rules expanded, with its action footer pinned while the content scrolls, and every other popup keeps its §11.6 reference size; one glyph registry in `ui/glyphs.rs` maps every icon drawn as a tinted glyph to a single private-use code point and is installed once at startup, so every icon draws on the first frame and no view registers glyphs of its own

- **invariants:** [discovered from T16 and from the wave-3 integration of T12, T13, T16 and T17 on 2026-11-23: spec §11.6 gives New scan 640 × 778 with matching rules expanded, while T12's `DialogKind::reference_size` gives one size per kind, so T16 landed with the expanded content scrolling inside a 640 × 528 host and the footer not pinned; each leg registers its own private-use glyphs (shell U+E100–E103, T13 U+E110–E116, T16 U+E130–E132, T17 U+E170–E171), so the check and chevron icons are registered more than once, and T16's popups draw no icon on the pass that registers them and request another frame, because egui activates a glyph rasterizer only from the next pass; the hook's size is an initial size and stays viewport-constrained (REQ-032; §11.6 "grow or internally scroll … keep the action footer reachable"); each icon has exactly one code point and each code point one icon; `add_glyph_rasterizer` is called only in `ui/glyphs.rs`, and a structural test makes any later registration elsewhere fail the gate; `FuzzyDedupeApp::new` installs the registry before the first pass, and a test harness that builds its context without `FuzzyDedupeApp::new` calls the same install function; an SVG that fails to parse is logged and its glyph left out, never drawn; invalidates: none, because no MS-1 evidence record exists yet]

with that orientation T14's widget would draw the target on the left and Compare would put Queue B over the counterpart, and every fix inside T15's files either leaves Queue B refused with `EvidenceMismatch` or queues the left-pane file under a "Queue B" label, a safety defect in a tool that moves files to Trash; for every pair, Findings' Unique value on the target's row, `only_in_b`, the `ReviewedPair` count and Review unique text's count are one number (one measure, C-04); the design scenario's `last_compared` pair shows A, the reference, on the left; no evidence-key or C-05 change; the inventory is the base's `git grep -n -E 'only_in_a|only_in_b|copy_only_in|Side A|side A' -- crates/` plus the side reads `aligned.a` in `unique_text_review.rs` and `aligned.a` and `aligned.b` in `tests/review_popups.rs`, and its hits in `ui/findings.rs` ("inside Actions") and `tests/no_file_mutation.rs` (`flags_macro_rules_only_in_allowlisted_files`) are false matches left untouched; - **proof:** [PV-T36-001]

#### T38: Keep the focused popup control visible above the pinned footer

- **disposition:** active
- **outcome:** whenever a popup body control has keyboard focus, it lies entirely inside the body's visible clip, scrolled into view when needed, at every viewport size from 1024 × 600 up and for any body length, whether focus arrived by Tab, by Shift+Tab or was moved there by the popup itself (Review unique text's acknowledgment checkbox after an unacknowledged Queue for cleanup), and a pointer click at the focused control's centre activates it
- **work_type:** behavior
- **checkpoint:** one green commit with the required `Plan-*` checkpoint trailers, landed by squash
- **boundary:** cross-task
- **depends_on:** [T36]
- **dependency_reason:** T38 corrects the scroll behavior of the body that T36's pinned footer slot in the T12-owned `ui/widgets/dialog_host.rs` leaves above the footer, and T17's acknowledgment focus move in the T12-owned `ui/dialogs/unique_text_review.rs`, which T36 also modified; the defect exists only once T36 pins the footer, so T36 is the prerequisite, and T17, T16, T18 and T19 precede T36 already; T38 lands before T22's checkpoint, which the orchestrator enforces without changing T22's producer-owned `depends_on`; proof construction: `integration_queue_entry_points_agree_on_a_counterpart_of_a_queued_file` stays T22's PV-T22-007 case in T22's `tests/integration.rs`, while T38 owns a focused regression in `tests/popup_focus.rs`, so T38's checkpoint is observable on its own and no file has two owners
- **requirements:** [REQ-032]
- **proof:** [PV-T38-001]
- **source_refs:** [request, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#116-popup-contracts-and-compact-progress-handling, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#72-non-functional-requirements, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#1110-resizing-scaling-and-keyboard-behavior]
- **consumes:** [C-12, one pinned action-footer slot in the C-12 dialog host, used by every popup that has an action footer, form popups, review popups, cleanup dialog, info popups]
- **produces:** [the C-12 dialog host's visible focus for popup bodies: a focused body control lies inside the body's visible clip, with no change to C-12's Appendix A text]
- **preserves:** [T36's pinned footer slot, every footer control's position inside the frame, the 16-unit content inset and the visible clip, and the body-then-footer Tab order, with PV-T36-001 passing unmodified; T34's size hook and every popup's §11.6 reference size, with PV-T34-001 passing unmodified; C-12 at most one popup, Esc cancel, the non-destructive Enter default and focus restoration to the invoker; Review cleanup's initial focus on Cancel; T17's acknowledgment flow: Queue for cleanup stays enabled, and pressed without the acknowledgment it queues nothing, shows the inline prompt under the checkbox and moves focus to the checkbox; every popup body's content, controls and their order; `dialog_host::mode_of` and `matching_rules::check_box` unchanged in signature and behavior, because T37's files import them]
- **invariants:** [discovered from T22's assembly leg (leg commit `f3aceb1`, branch `t22-assemble`) on 2026-11-24, measured at 1600 × 960 on Review unique text for `Requirements_v2.txt` (9 unique lines), popup frame y 269 to 691: after T36 pinned the footer (Queue for cleanup at y 644 to 674), the acknowledgment checkbox "I reviewed the unique text in this file." is laid out at y 649 to 669, below the body's visible clip and under the pinned footer's band; pressing Queue for cleanup without the acknowledgment shows the prompt and moves keyboard focus to the checkbox, as spec §11.6 requires, but the checkbox stays scrolled out of view, so focus sits on a control the user cannot see, against §11.6's visible focus and NFR-005's "every action is reachable by keyboard with visible focus"; a pointer click there cannot reach it, so the user cannot tick it by pointer without first scrolling; T22's `integration_queue_entry_points_agree_on_a_counterpart_of_a_queued_file` is red for this reason (Row menu outcome "Review first" against Checkbox outcome "Counterpart of a queued file"); settled design (orchestrator, 2026-11-24): the rule is a visible-focus guarantee for every popup body, covering focus moved by Tab, by Shift+Tab and by the popup itself, at every viewport size from 1024 × 600 up and for any body length; ownership: T38 owns the focus-visibility regression in `tests/popup_focus.rs`, and the red T22 case stays T22's PV-T22-007 case; T38 changes rendering, so T22's snapshot baselines are generated on the runner only after T38 lands; invalidates: [EV-001], because T38 changes non-test code under `crates/`, so T27 renews or reviews EV-001 in EV-007 (D-016)]

#### T39: Start the app on a named fixture scenario for manual checks

- **disposition:** active
- **outcome:** a normal launch, outside measurement mode, with `FUZZY_DEDUPE_SCENARIO` set to a scenario name starts on that fixture scenario, and New scan replays it; the name is one of `ScenarioKind::from_name`'s names (`large`, `mixed`, `issues`, `empty`, `unicode`, `stale`, `design`); unset, the app starts on `design` exactly as before; an unknown or empty value writes one standard-error line naming the valid names, logs a `tracing` warning and starts on `design`; the value is read once per process; with `FUZZY_DEDUPE_MEASURE` also set, measurement mode's scenario wins and a standard-error line says the scenario variable was ignored; preferences load and are written as for any normal launch
- **work_type:** behavior
- **checkpoint:** one green commit with the required `Plan-*` checkpoint trailers, landed by squash
- **boundary:** cross-task
- **depends_on:** [T23]
- **dependency_reason:** T39 corrects the startup scenario selection that T23 filled into the T12-owned `measure/mod.rs` (`startup_for`, `startup` and `startup_scenario`, which choose only between measurement mode's scenario and `design`), and its precedence rule needs T23's measurement configuration, so T23 is the prerequisite; T12's `FuzzyDedupeApp::with_startup` adoption path and T16's New scan replay through `measure::startup_scenario` precede T23 already; T39 lands before T26's checkpoint, because T26's C-13 rows for Japanese, Arabic, emoji and long paths need the `unicode` scenario, and before the T24 and T25 checkpoints, whose records must bind a tree that carries T39 (D-016); the orchestrator enforces that order without changing the producer-owned `depends_on` of T24, T25 or T26
- **requirements:** [REQ-018]
- **proof:** [PV-T39-001]
- **source_refs:** [request, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#19-implementation-plan, spec:docs/specs/canonical/fuzzy-dedupe.md#1111-acceptance-checklist-for-the-implementing-agent]
- **consumes:** [C-10 sample stream, app module stubs, engine module stubs]
- **produces:** [a developer startup-scenario selection through `FUZZY_DEDUPE_SCENARIO` in the `measure` startup path, the single source of the startup scenario, with no change to C-10's or C-12's Appendix A text]
- **preserves:** [a launch without `FUZZY_DEDUPE_SCENARIO` starts on `design` and renders exactly as before, so T22's snapshot baselines stand; measurement mode's activation, parsing, scenarios, drivers, C-10 sample stream and preference isolation, with `measure::active()` true only in measurement mode and PV-T23-001 passing unmodified; preferences load and write on every normal launch, with PV-T20-001 passing unmodified; `FuzzyDedupeApp::new` adopting `measure::startup()` through `with_startup`, and New scan replaying `measure::startup_scenario()`, both unchanged in signature and behavior; the fixture scenarios and `ScenarioKind`'s names unchanged]
- **invariants:** [discovered while preparing T26's EV-004 checklist (leg commit `a1f5059`, branch `t26-pass`) on 2026-11-24: the shipped binary always starts on `design`; measurement mode (`FUZZY_DEDUPE_MEASURE`) reaches only `large`, the large pair and `design`, and it scripts its own input, quits by itself and ignores preferences, so it cannot serve a manual pass; `ScenarioKind::from_name` in `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-engine/src/fixtures/mod.rs`, documented as scenario selection for developers (T4's executor discretion left the mechanism open), is called only from tests; so T26's C-13 rows for Japanese, Arabic, emoji and long paths (the `unicode` scenario: Japanese, Arabic, emoji and decomposed-accent names, a long path, mixed line endings) cannot be exercised, nor can the `issues`, `empty`, `stale`, `large` and `mixed` states; settled design (orchestrator, 2026-11-24): the variable is a developer and manual-check affordance, read once per process; the message listing valid names derives them from `ScenarioKind`'s own list, never a typed copy; a value that is not valid Unicode is treated as an unknown value, as `FUZZY_DEDUPE_MEASURE` treats one; preferences are never isolated by this variable, which isolation stays measurement mode's alone; tests never mutate the process environment, which `unsafe_code = "deny"` and edition 2024's unsafe `set_var` rule out; `app.rs` needs no change, and a change there, including to `FuzzyDedupeApp::new`'s doc comment, is a recorded excursion, reported at the checkpoint; T39 proves the in-app precondition for REQ-018's Unicode and long-path items only, and the manual pass itself stays T26's PV-T26-002; invalidates: [EV-001, EV-002, EV-006], because T39 changes non-test code under `crates/`, so T27 renews or reviews each in EV-007 (D-016); default-launch rendering is unchanged, so T24's captures are re-rendered on the `main` that carries T39 and compared by hash, and the `ui-test` job reruns on every `main` push]
- **executor_discretion:** [the shape of the parse and decision function (preferred: a pure function of the measurement configuration and the scenario value, so tests need no environment mutation); where the standard-error wording lives; test names after the `startup_scenario` prefix]
- **files:** [`crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/src/measure/mod.rs` (modify: startup scenario selection in `startup()` and `startup_for`, the single source of the startup scenario, only, reading the measurement configuration that drives the C-10 sample stream without changing it; owner T12), `crates/fuzzy-dedupe-app/tests/startup_scenario.rs` (create; owner T39)]
- **parallel_safe:** yes
- **conflicts_with:** [T12, T23]
- **supersedes:** []
- **superseded_by:** []
- **corrects:** [T23]
- **discovered_from:** [T26]

- **recovery:** revert to the last green checkpoint; the variable's parse, its precedence rule and its standard-error notices revert together, so no launch is left reading the variable without reporting an unknown value; a revert of T39 blocks T26's Unicode and long-path C-13 rows again until a new correction task lands
- **acceptance:** PV-T39-001 proves (1) the decision function: unset gives `Design`; each valid name gives its `ScenarioKind`; an unknown or empty value gives `Design` with a message listing every valid name, derived from the source of truth and not typed out; a measurement configuration takes precedence over any scenario value, with a note when both are present; and without a scenario value, measurement mode's choices are unchanged; (2) that an app started through the same adoption path on `unicode` shows the scenario's Japanese, Arabic, emoji and long-path names in Findings, and New scan's replay uses `unicode`; (3) that a normal start with a scenario chosen still loads `preferences.toml` and writes a changed preference, as `tests/preferences.rs` shows for the default start; and (4) that the existing `measure` and `preferences` suites pass unchanged and the repository gates are green
- **sub-tasks:**
  - **T39.1 RED** — add `tests/startup_scenario.rs` with the acceptance (1) to (3) cases, and the decision function's signature with the base behavior (the scenario value ignored) so the suite compiles; expected failure: every valid name other than `design` yields `Design`, an unknown value yields no message, and the `unicode` start shows the `design` rows.
  - **T39.2 Verify RED** — run `rexec -- cargo nextest run -p fuzzy-dedupe-app --locked -E 'test(startup_scenario)'` and confirm the failures are those, not build errors.
  - **T39.3 GREEN** — read `FUZZY_DEDUPE_SCENARIO` once per process and select the startup scenario in `measure/mod.rs` by the settled rule.
  - **T39.4 Verify GREEN** — rerun the targeted tests, `rexec -- cargo nextest run -p fuzzy-dedupe-app --locked -E 'test(measure) | test(preferences)'`, the whole app test suite, the full nextest gate and the Clippy gate.
  - **T39.5 REFACTOR** — assess and record none.
  - **T39.6 Verify Task** — run PV-T39-001 and the repository gates; commit with the required `Plan-*` trailers.

T13's Findings toolbar, filter row, progress track, table and row menus in `ui/findings.rs` and `ui/widgets/grouped_table.rs`; T15's Compare toolbar, change row, identity header and wrap default in `ui/compare.rs` and the Compare fields of `presentation/state.rs`, with T14's `ui/widgets/diff_view.rs` only if needed; T17's acknowledgment checkbox in `ui/dialogs/unique_text_review.rs`; and T22's `tests/snapshots.rs` and its baselines; T22 is a prerequisite because it owns the rig and baselines T40 corrects and depends on T13 and T15 to T19, and T38 because it is the last task to modify `unique_text_review.rs` and, through T36, the last to modify `cleanup.rs`, `keyboard_shortcuts.rs`, `matching_rules.rs` and `new_scan.rs`, so every corrected task precedes T40 in the graph; - **invariants:** [discovered from T24's design review (capture leg `e68070f`, branch `t24-review`) on 2026-11-24, triaged in the execution log `T24-triage.md`: deviations from the review frames in most of the 19 entries; §11.3's collapse step 3 ("secondary toolbar actions move into the Actions menu"; the toolbar row's "overflow secondary actions at smaller sizes") is not implemented, so at 200% zoom and at the 1024 × 600 minimum toolbars, filter rows, change rows and status bars overprint or run off canvas, against §11.3 ("should not be enforced by clipping content under zoom") and NFR-005; Compare's identity header has no keyboard route to the full path, against §11.3's "accessible full path" and §11.10's "keyboard-reachable details and copy actions; hover alone is insufficient"; CI's snapshot rig draws inside kittest's 8-unit outer margin, so EV-006's frames do not show the window as drawn; precedence (§11.1): explicit owner decisions and safety, then the §11 compact-UI contract, then the current frames, and where §11 is silent presentation follows the frames; business rules are never inferred from decorative elements or sample data, so frame sample values are layout and wording references only; owner decision 2026-11-24 (recorded in spec 0.13's Scan setup glossary row and IR-003): a fresh scan setup selects every supported file type, which under C-013 is every decodable plain-text file, so an empty extension list means no extension filter, New scan shows it as "All plain-text files", Start does not require a file type, and the C-08 schema does not change; MS-1's simulated scan reads no file types (only `new_scan.rs` and `presentation/state.rs` refer to them), so the rule needs no engine change in MS-1; settled items by leg (orchestrator, 2026-11-24), each file owned by exactly one leg: leg S — S1 the shell allocates the shared right-hand items (Review cleanup, the Matching rules gear) first and gives the workspace toolbar the remaining rect, so a workspace's `ui.available_width()` is its whole budget, the Review cleanup label shortens to "Cleanup (N)" when the budget is tight (frame `29:3648`) and the gear always stays visible; S2 "Review cleanup (N)" is disabled when N is 0 (frame `28:1399`); S3 the rail's Compare entry is disabled when no pair exists (§11.2); S4 main-menu items sit at a 28-unit pitch, egui's item spacing not stacking on the row height, by T37's technique; S5 the status bar is one line that never overprints, its left text eliding or yielding to the right text by a stated priority; S6 in Compare the status line shows a Compare line structured as frame `13:1304`'s ("G004 · 3 files · A is the comparison reference …" left, "Read-only · Unique text on both sides" right), its content derived from state and not from the frame's sample values; leg F — F1 the Findings toolbar fits its budget, the search field shrinking to a stated minimum before step 3 moves secondary actions into the Actions menu in a stated priority order; F2 the search glyph sits inside the field and the text is vertically centred in the 30-unit field; F3 filter chips 26 high with the frame's padding and spacing; F4 the caption "Match and Unique are measured …" is width-checked, eliding or hiding when it does not fit and never overprinting the chips, as `compare.rs` already checks a similar caption; F5 the progress track is visible, 360 × 6 with an animated indeterminate segment (frame `28:2171`), inventing no total, which the spec does not list; F6 the table header row shows on the empty states (frames `28:1399` and `30:2370`); F7 the ⋯ row-menu button shows on every member row (frame `13:648`); F8 File name and Folder are flexible (§11.4) and shrink at narrow widths and at zoom so the Decision column stays on canvas, the Folder text shortening visually (for example "~/…/Projects/", frame `29:2214`) without changing the stored or copied value; F9 the Protected value uses the Protected (green) token; F10 the Queue header shows in full; F11 no vertical cell rules, and the reference row carries the frame's anchor icon and bold name; F12 the full path shows when a row or its Folder cell has keyboard focus, not only on hover, beside the existing Copy path; leg C — C1 the Compare toolbar fits its budget, secondary actions (Keep both, Queue B for cleanup…, the candidate stepper if needed) moving into an overflow menu in a stated priority order, with ‹ Findings staying; C2 the change row is width-checked, so Linked scroll, Wrap and the Original / Normalized switch never overlap and are never cut, and may wrap or overflow into the row's own menu; C3 the B role ("B · Review first") uses the amber token; C4 the Normalized segment is not clipped; C5 the identity header shows the full path when it has keyboard focus, gaining keyboard focusability if it lacks it, and offers Copy path as a header action or a stated chord; C6 wrap defaults on (frame `13:1304`), with an existing preference still winning; leg P1 — P1 cleanup rows are 51 high (the code's own `ROW_HEIGHT`) with content vertically centred, the executor finding why the layout loses it; P2 a 28-unit header band; P3 footer button labels vertically centred, a cause in the shared footer code of `dialog_host.rs` being reported as an excursion and not edited; P4 at zoom the columns fit the popup body or scroll internally (§11.6 "grow or internally scroll"), with no column off the body; P5 the full path shows on keyboard focus of a row; leg P2 — P6 Keyboard shortcuts shows one key cap per chord (§11.6), so multi-chord rows render several caps and no cap runs past its column at 200%, and adds the frame's "Keyboard shortcuts F1 / Ctrl+?" row; P7 the Matching rules similarity field is about 95 wide; P8 Review unique text's acknowledgment checkbox is 16 units (§11.7); P9 New scan's file types follow the owner decision, an empty list meaning all plain-text files, the summary reading "All plain-text files", clearing the edit field returning to it, and `blocking_problem` no longer requiring a file type; leg R, after every other leg is integrated — R1 the rig draws into `content_rect` through `new_child`, exactly as `tests/layout.rs` does; R2 the New scan frame seeds a scan setup (folders, protection, file types) matching frame `16:806`'s structure; R3 the Scanning frame clears the results and queue, as Start scan does (FR-026); R4 the Findings frames (`13:648`, `37:2466`, `44:3954`) reach the frame's view state, G003, G002, G004 and G009 expanded and Design_Spec_draft focused; R5 all 32 baselines are regenerated through the CI `ui-snapshots` artifact and reviewed against the frames before they are committed; baselines come only from the snapshot runner, as T22's invariant requires; T40 changes rendering, so the baselines are regenerated only after legs S, F, C, P1 and P2 are integrated; invalidates: [EV-001, EV-002, EV-006], because T40 changes non-test code under `crates/`: EV-002 is re-recorded by T24's rerun on the `main` that carries T40, EV-006 is renewed through PV-T40-002's passing `ui-test` run on T40's tree or by T27, and T27 renews or reviews EV-001 in EV-007 (D-016); T24, T26 and T27 keep their own proofs, and T40 proves only the in-app preconditions REQ-018 and REQ-012 need]
